[24:19] When you start a computer up, the very first thing that happens is it runs a power on self-test or a post.

[25:54] There are other bootloaders that you can use, including the Linux loader, which is Lilo.
[26:00] You can use either of those bootloaders, but basically the point of the bootloader is to actually boot the operating system from the hard disk.
[26:10] And it does this by reading the first section of the hard drive.
[26:16] So with the master boot record, it is the first 512 bytes, which is the first sector of the hard drive, or the first block of the hard drive.
[26:25] And with the GUID partition table, we're talking about a much larger section.
[26:31] We've got larger partition identifiers and a lot more of them with the GUID partition table.
[26:37] But basically, we are looking at the very first blocks on the hard drive
[26:43] in order to not only look for the partitions or volumes that are associated with a particular operating system,
[26:50] but also where the boot code is located.
[26:53] Now the boot code of course is different from the operating system.
[26:56] The boot code is really just there to locate the operating system and then read that into memory.

[05:36:04] In order to have an IPTables rule, you need to have something that you are going to apply that rule to.
